## Current native depth publication4 (2026-10-15)

Source2 observes exactly one authentic completed control4 D32Float cell.
Publication4 is available only on Vulkan, with no shader execution grant;
Null validates/refuses, Metal and WebGPU explicitly refuse this profile.
Publication3 remains WebGPU/Source1/control2. ReadInput1 rejects Source2, so
neither existing WebGPU execution5 nor a native Root reader inherits authority.
Real public GraphicsExecution1 produces the cell through the original Content9
journal and fence, with cancel/retry, stale-proof refusal and retired descriptor
refresh. Independent RenderDoc confirms actual depth values on the same
resource for both submitted draws, without validation messages. See
bindless-sampled-depth-contract.md for the exact scope and evidence.

## Public WebGPU implicit-reference parity (2026-10-15; scoped Backend Validated)

The public OriginalExecution3/4 Root outputs now match an independent ordinary
Traditional implicit-sampling reference for RGBA8 Texture2D and Texture2DArray.
The ordinary Slang shaders use static texture/sampler bindings and real Sample
derivatives before non-uniform material selection and array discard. The Root
computes gradients of the same UV before dispatch/discard and uses sampleGrad.
All sixteen output bytes match both clean public Root receipts in each cohort,
including the true non-base mip. Two uploaded textures, independent nearest/
linear samplers, two mips, and one/two layers share the documented input ABI.

The explicit ordinary compiler policy --preserve-implicit-sampling propagates
to sealed selected entries. Combined/Fragment WGSL retain textureSample and
SPIR-V retains OpImageSampleImplicitLod. A re-sealed legacy fixed-LOD shader and
matching base-level receipts are rejected by negative tests. Native derivative
uniformity validation remains enabled. The test-only reference creates no RHI
execution capability; original public C++ inventory, journal, genuine completion,
fault and retirement remain authoritative. Its independent ordinary device,
buffers and textures are destroyed after real queue/map and three error scopes.

Current integrated checks: CTest3/3 (two browser cohorts plus five-case reference
contract); public Root44 clean/fault lifecycle flows and8 numerical receipts;
source415/415; routing13/13; browser-process9/9. Both cohorts report hot0,
retirement quotas0 and no native errors/diagnostics. Exact durations, sealed
reference roles, source/binary hashes, snapshots and readbacks are archived at
D:/arp/build/bindless-original-buffer-sources1/root-web-implicit-parity-current-report.json.
The intentionally aborted first attempt (staged-script/source mismatch) remains
separate evidence. Browser assets now stage independently of the Wasm link.
Baseline-only Wasm and assertion-enabled O1 correctness evidence do not establish
default compiler or Release performance readiness.

## Authenticated finite buffer range publication1 (2026-10-15; scoped Backend Validated)

The existing OriginalBufferRangeDescriptor1 typed intent now permits WebGPU CPU
descriptor publication against authentic original leases. Null remains validator,
Vulkan retains native publication and Metal explicitly refuses. Publication alone
grants no shader, command or completion authority. Generic finite writes and
ordinary native consumers remain closed.

Every finite write uses schema2 with queried device uniform/storage alignment and
maximum binding sizes; dynamic offsets and insufficient/out-of-bounds intervals
reject before publication. The backend copies a candidate write list and matching
original lease publications; only a successful retained-metadata factory commits
all lists. It verifies native payload ownership against the actual registry.
Subsequent whole metadata bindings cannot erase the authenticated finite views.

Public OriginalExecution2/Preparation2 consumes these independent typed range
publications through the same snapshot and Root: cold physical group creation
matches native metadata to the successful immutable L2 write, checks actual
pipeline minimum and queried limits, and emits the exact offset and size. A native
layout minimum0 is a deferred use check; the authenticated generated table ABI
provides its independent nonzero minimum. Neither value is invented. It does
not enlarge the view or change resource identity. The snapshot metadata records
the finite runtime element count, and canonical state/journal/completion uses the
same access interval and original owners. No new execution version or general
capability is inferred from this independent publication contract.

## Fallback rules

- `ExtensionOptIn` is not a base capability. `EngineFallbackPlan::applyTo`
  disables it and reports that explicit opt-in is required.
- CPU replay is never selected by a default descriptor. A caller must set
  `SecondaryCommandEncoderDesc::allowCpuReplay` after selecting a compatible
  lowering contract.
- `NativeSync` is native only on backends that provide the L2 fence/semaphore
  semantics. Metal requires its queried, executable shared-event synchronization
  contract; absent support fails closed. WebGPU remains diagnostic. Frame-lifetime
  completion alone is not a substitute for L2 NativeSync.
- Vulkan `TimelineSync` requires a queried per-device capability contract and
  a queried, enabled timeline-semaphore feature in the same native runtime.
  The factory rejects a static backend declaration or synthetic runtime even
  when the Vulkan API vocabulary contains timeline semaphores.
- Failure to allocate a native aliasing heap fails closed. It must not be
  substituted with a Dummy heap under a native-memory contract.

## Backend matrix

ContentPlan2 consumes the existing original transaction root, frozen pass
arguments and State texture footprints. It projects every attachment Begin/End
and actual shader consumption at its original command ordinal, including multiple
attachments at one ordinal. Render bindings and barriers do not initialize
contents. Exact registered mip/layer/aspect cells retain their original content
observations; Load/read require prior stored coverage, Clear remains tentative
until Store, and Discard removes coverage. Projection work, cells, steps and
controls have independent explicit ceilings. The existing single MutationJournal
owns CPU state and content proposals together; rollback restores all cells but
never recycles issued generations. CPU publication cannot claim GPU completion.
Null validates intent only; Vulkan/Metal selected original joins remain absent;
WebGPU admits the explicitly opted-in CPU plan only. Native recording, content
execution and completion are independent closed gates.

ContentCreation2 extends the existing exclusive texture-content creation domain
with fixed descriptor-authored mip/layer/aspect cells, explicit cell/mutation/CPU
ceilings and original registration-bound range observations. Null is validator-only
and refuses resource creation; Vulkan/Metal diagnose missing selected payload;
WebGPU permits explicitly opted-in sealed cold resource/content storage only.
Native initialization, ContentPlan2 execution and completion remain unavailable
on all four backends; version1 whole-texture upload receipts cannot authorize it.

ContentCreation2 permits descriptor-authored CopySrc on its attachment domain,
so the same original output can eventually be read back. This is a creation
permission only: it issues no initialized-content receipt or copy/readback
execution authority. CopyDst and Storage remain outside this profile. Null
validates these descriptors only; Vulkan/Metal selected creation remains
DiagnosticFailure; WebGPU preserves exclusive content policy on native cold
creation. Ordinary descriptor, transfer and readback gates remain unchanged.

The selected WebGPU table CPU composition now consumes canonical Creation2 depth
parents with independently registered attachment views and a canonical CopySrc
color target through the same Inputs4/State3/Stream3/ContentPlan2 root. Explicit
viewport/scissor and three ordered Clear/Store passes precede conservative table
consumption. Usage2/Dependency2 scope and hazard projection, original proposals,
rollback and loss retirement validate on genuine resources. Null remains a
value validator; Vulkan/Metal selected original creation joins remain diagnosed.
WebGPU native freeze/custody/recording/submission/completion remain unavailable;
successful cold bind-group creation and CPU projection grant no public Emulated.

ContentCellSequence2 is the allocation-free value timeline prerequisite for
ContentPlan2. Null validates values only; Vulkan/Metal diagnose absent selected
original joins; WebGPU admits CPU validation only. Clear is tentative until its
matching pass Store, Load needs prior stored coverage, Discard removes coverage,

resource generation, exact mip/layer/aspect and bounded visits/steps remain
mandatory. No original ownership, mutation, texel initialization, native recording
or completion is inferred from a successful value timeline.

Null validates value intent only; Vulkan and Metal reject the selected original

retains Projection2 CPU clauses only. Freeze1/Closure2 cannot consume the new
profile. Recording, GPU initialization, submission and completion remain closed;
public Emulated is unavailable. This increment's execution validation is pending.

The validated Vulkan BindlessCross subset consumes a device-issued
authorization through public snapshot, compute/render-pipeline and pass
extensions. Its Compute operations are limited to the real six-resource-class
fixture. Direct Graphics validates sampled-texture `load/query` in Vertex and
`load/query/sampleLevel/sampleGrad` plus indexed samplers in Fragment. Both paths
use completion-gated fault reports and GPU readback. Native Vertex fault
reporting is authorized only when Vulkan reports the relevant indexing and
vertex storage-side-effect features enabled. Single indexed indirect is
validated with an enabled first-instance feature. Count/multi-draw, Metal
Graphics/indirect and image domains outside MetalComputeImages/1, WebGPU graphics stages/operations outside the tested Fragment subset,
and WOCC remain unavailable. This bounded Vulkan result does not satisfy the
cross-backend contract.

Metal's public Compute buffer subset is scoped Backend Validated on Apple M4
with Metal API Validation. Explicit opt-in is independent from ordinary
argument-buffer support; native tier 2, shared-event synchronization and queried
buffer length limits are prerequisites. The generated Slang/SPIR-V/MSL and
reflection publication is consumed through device-issued authorization and
immutable prepared bindings. Independent data readbacks prove non-uniform
load/query/store/atomic operations, Clean/Faulted reports, suppressed invalid
accesses, preparation/command budgets and completion-owned retention. Prepared
native rebinding and direct dispatch add no core allocations; fault-readback
preparation and submission completion owners still allocate. Evidence is
`build/mac-metal-debug/artifacts/bindless-metal/results.xml`. No Graphics,
renderer/WOCC, full Platform Validated or Production Ready claim follows.

MetalGraphicsBuffers/1 admission is Contract Complete as a separate preflight,
sharing buffer-domain validation with Compute and consuming sealed compiler ABI
metadata. Its tested operations are Vertex/Fragment UniformBuffer load and
ReadOnlyStorageBuffer load/query, plus Fragment StorageBuffer query/atomicAdd.
Other operations, resource classes, mixed Compute stages and extra domains
reject; admission grants no capability. Private native Graphics scope evidence
includes exact ordered intent, attachment Load/Store and Vertex-only depth,
multiple real scopes sharing bounded quotas, completed Faulted-to-Clean domain
reuse and exact completion ownership. Public Graphics capability versions
remain zero until device-issued authorization, the L2 adapter and capture gates
pass. Evidence: `build/mac-metal-debug/artifacts/bindless-prepared-graphics/`
`scope-report.yaml`, `composition-report.yaml` and `admission-report.yaml`.
RenderDoc is unavailable on this host (`RHI-CAPTURE-RENDERDOC-TOOL-UNAVAILABLE`);
no capture, visual acceptance, MinGW, full platform or production claim follows.

MetalRetainedGraphicsPipeline/1 now owns selected-entry interfaces, full domain
layouts and sealed package bytes; the exact Vertex and optional Fragment maps
consume their separate verified artifacts without file IO. Its private builder
is scoped Backend Validated by exact native pixel/depth/fault smokes. The typed
Graphics binding adapter reuses canonical native preparation, preserves owner
count/byte leases and rejects real Validator/foreign/missing owners. Its source
and rejection boundary is Contract Complete; public typed-owner preparation,
command consumption and completion are not Backend Validated. Shared capability
predicates keep all public Graphics versions zero, including with real Compute
authorization. `factory-report.yaml` and `adapter-report.yaml` in the same
evidence directory record these separate scopes. Null remains Validator and
Vulkan/WebGPU additional prepared Graphics remain DiagnosticFailure/version 0.

Indexed indirect dispatch remains closed on all four backends, regardless of
the direct Compute snapshot or ordinary indirect capability. It needs a
versioned argument-range/access, producer-order and completion contract.
Metal/Vulkan use the existing WebGPU diagnostic
`RHI-BINDLESS-INDIRECT-DISPATCH-UNSUPPORTED`; Null retains
`RHI-BINDLESS-INDIRECT-CONTRACT-UNAVAILABLE`. Metal's public native negative gate
passes; the Vulkan change has source compilation evidence only on this Apple
host, with native/MinGW regression pending.

WebGPU publishes Emulated capability only for the non-uniform Compute operations
and Fragment sampled-texture, `Slang.float4.uniform/1` UniformBuffer, and
`Slang.uint32.structured/1` ReadOnlyStorageBuffer and StorageBuffer operations
listed above. Fragment StorageBuffer writes require exact read/write access
declarations and have completion-gated readback of distinct atomic and store
effects in the public RHI fixture.
The Emscripten/Chrome public RHI fixtures verify package authorization,
immutable snapshot recording, declared texture and buffer access ranges, queue
completion, Clean generation fault reports, compute result readback, and
red/green graphics pixel readback. Other resource classes, operations, formats,
ABIs and graphics stages remain unavailable until their public execution
fixtures pass. This is backend evidence for the tested operation subset, not
WOCC workload, platform-wide, or production readiness.

For Vulkan surface-backed `ShutdownDrain/1`, a graphics submission fence alone
cannot prove that `vkQueuePresentKHR` has consumed its wait semaphore. Native
completion requires a queried and enabled `VK_KHR_swapchain_maintenance1`
feature and its enabled `VK_KHR_surface_maintenance1` and
`VK_KHR_get_surface_capabilities2` instance-extension dependencies, a
backend-owned fence supplied for every present (including frame
discard), bounded retention of those fences, and successful waits for both
submitted commands and present fences against the same absolute deadline.
The runtime must reject missing extension/feature, allocation or capacity
failure, and incomplete or failed waits with a stable diagnostic. A device
may report `Native/Completed` only after both queues' relevant work has been
observed complete; it may not replace the deadline with `vkQueueWaitIdle`.
Surface-backed resize uses the same absolute-deadline submission/present drain
before reusing frame-slot semaphores. A failed drain leaves the current
swapchain intact. Vulkan's `oldSwapchain` replacement may retire the old chain
as soon as native creation succeeds, so a later candidate failure closes
native acquire/present rather than silently falling back to synthetic frames.

`docs/bindless-cross-contract.md` specifies the indexed-resource extension
and intended Native/Emulated mappings. Vulkan publishes the bounded Compute,
direct Graphics and single indexed-indirect subsets backed by public package
authorization, command consumption and completion-gated fault reporting.
All unvalidated backend paths remain unavailable.

The shared L2 indirect-read validator checks a full `DrawIndexedIndirectCommand`
range, buffer usage, and an exact `DrawIndirect` / `IndirectCommandRead`
declaration. It has assertion-enabled positive and negative tests. Null copies
bounded indirect declarations at snapshot bind and consumes the validator at indexed
indirect draw, then rejects execution with
`RHI-BINDLESS-INDIRECT-CONTRACT-UNAVAILABLE`. Its encoder reservation is
`maxBindlessIndirectAccesses`; exceeding it returns
`RHI-BINDLESS-INDIRECT-ACCESS-BUDGET`. Vulkan now retains the same declaration
through a single indexed indirect draw, requires enabled
`drawIndirectFirstInstance`, and executes the native command with fence,
Clean fault report and independent pixel readback. WebGPU also retains the
same declaration and executes a single indexed indirect record when the created device enabled `indirect-first-instance`. The
index-buffer bind is encoded before the draw; missing native binding rejects
with `RHI-WEBGPU-INDIRECT-INDEX-BUFFER-UNBOUND`. The Chrome public fixture
requires completion, Clean fault report, independent pixel readback and no
native errors. Multi-draw/count and Metal remain rejected. Passing the L2
or Null check alone cannot promote a backend capability.
WebGPU's `indirectFirstInstance` Bindless capability is read from the created
device's feature set. The Chrome public fixture tests both enabled and
explicitly disabled device states. Devices without the feature reject this path: nonzero `firstInstance`
records require a separate validated zero-base shader mapping before execution;
ordinary `IndirectDraw` availability does not grant that mapping.

`IndirectInstanceBase/1` now defines the shared bounded value encoder and
decoder: a 20-byte physical command has zero first instance, accompanied by a
16-byte versioned logical base/count/capacity record. Rejected GPU encodings
suppress instances and latch the index fault. CPU/Null validates the same
range contract; Vulkan and Metal retain their existing Native first-instance
contracts; WebGPU public Compute exercises the generated Slang encoder with
completion/readback. Ordinary no-feature indexed draws still reject. A package
version or successful encoder probe does not authorize mapping consumption by
a Vertex shader or an indirect command. The separately validated closed public
processor gate below authorizes only its protected packet.

`IndirectInstanceConsumer/1` now adds an independently gated scalar Vertex
reader with a fixed offline record index and exact logical capacity. Null
validates the mapping binding, full 16-byte record extent, plan identity and
Vertex access declarations. WebGPU's public package generates the mapping in
Compute, transitions it for Vertex reading and verifies a direct graphics
frame against the shared CPU reference. Six malformed mappings/local IDs
require Fragment fault forwarding, completion-gated Faulted reports and no
material storage writes or atomics. RGBA8 readback comparison permits one
quantization unit; diagnostic output consumes the same pre-branch gradients
so Slang cannot sink derivative evaluation after a non-uniform early return.
SPIR-V, MSL, WGSL and reflection compile through Arp's row-major pipeline.
Vulkan and Metal publish consumer version zero pending their own execution
evidence. The WebGPU consumer version grants neither arbitrary Vertex indexed
resources nor zero-base indirect drawing: command-producer correspondence and
backend-owned retention of validated physical arguments remain required.

`ZeroBaseIndirectProducer/1` is now a closed compiler entry with integer
parameters, guarded input range/capacity, unconditional zero physical first
instance and suppressed invalid output. Runtime provenance binds the complete
package payload to the same compiler/generator build, including targets,
reflection and lowering layout, so updating caller-owned hashes after target
substitution cannot certify execution. Builds without shader tools reject this
role using empty executable identities. Compilation/package evidence alone
cannot publish a producer execution version.

`BindlessIndirectPacket/1` has an independent protected-storage capability.
Null version 1 validates fixed-record-zero storage preparation, exact consumer
capacity, count/byte quotas, initialization and host/transfer/writable-descriptor
seals. Packet references retain their quotas after pool destruction; device
epoch expiry invalidates them. WebGPU version 1 additionally validates actual
native extents/usages, the issuing RHI device's registry membership, byte
accounting and exact initialized content through Chrome GPU readback. The
same native WGPUDevice cannot authorize another RHI device's ordinary host
writes. Vulkan and Metal keep storage version zero and reject preparation.
The common BindlessIndirectPacketStorage/1 revalidation now shares the exact
three-role descriptor schema with packet creation: distinct issued IDs, exact
20/16/16-byte logical extents/usages/CpuToGpu intent/initial states, all write
seals, live leases and overflow-safe aggregate accounting. Current recording
states remain independently journaled. Facts clear on failure; value checks
allocate0 and grant no native byte or same-issuer authority. All four backend
storage/processor capability versions and execution conclusions above remain
unchanged.

No packet here proves trusted GPU production,
producer/consumer ordering, snapshot correspondence or completion retention.
No-feature indexed recording therefore remains closed.

`BindlessIndirectProcessorIntent/1` now adds bounded immutable preparation and
recording ownership. Null validates it against the issuing resource registry;
WebGPU additionally validates the actual source buffer's extent/usage and exact
RHI registry payload. Vulkan/Metal reject preparation with
`RHI-BINDLESS-INDIRECT-PROCESSOR-PREPARATION-UNAVAILABLE`. The intent retains the
canonical closed producer package, source buffer, protected packet, graphics-only
consumer authorization, exact sealed snapshot and complete plan/generation.
Separate producer/consumer declaration views cover source/parameters, protected
copy destinations, Vertex mapping, indirect arguments and all snapshot accesses.
Shared owners are conservatively charged to fixed count/byte pools. Successful
recording validation has assertion-enabled and Chrome allocation checks;
descriptor liveness uses scalar registry facts without copying labels while
preserving current parent liveness and write seals. Compiler/package evidence
includes graphics-only Native and Emulated reader variants and rejects mixed
Compute consumers. All public producer execution versions remain zero. The
intent/lease is not an executable processor, does not bypass sealed writes and
does not prove submission/completion retention; no-feature drawing stays closed.

WebGPU's private `PreparedZeroBaseIndirectProducer/1` now uses an explicit
device-owned, fixed-capacity completion owner. Command preparation reserves a
generation ticket and producer/fault capacities; cancellation releases only
unsubmitted ownership. Submission validates matching producer fault readbacks
and transfers the preallocated callback owner through real queue completion.
An unproven callback result closes admission and quarantines its storage;
the exact native device's lost promise provides terminal recovery. Core/Null
tests validate the bounded state machine, and Chrome separately verifies
successful in-flight retention and actual device-destroy/lost observation.
Unknown completion-result injection is ownership coverage only, not evidence
of a real failing GPU queue. Producer execution version remains zero on all
four public extensions; storage/preparation evidence cannot open zero-base
drawing.

The Vulkan-private `BindlessFaultSubmission/1` lowering now uses a preamble
command buffer ordered before the workload command buffer in the same queue
submission. It is retained with the workload until the same native fence
completes. The raw-buffer preamble fixture remains backend-private; the public
BindlessCross compute fixture separately proves snapshot-owned clear/readback
ordering. Other backends must implement the same ordering or reject indexed
recording.

`GpuBuffer::sealWrites()` is a monotonic L2 ownership restriction using the
existing host/descriptor/transfer seals. Null, Vulkan, Metal and WebGPU preserve
the same rejection contract. Any owner can tighten protection; this method
grants no privileged writer and has no unseal operation. Closed processors may
write only through their separately validated backend-private lowering.

`PreparedIndirectProcessor/1` specifies the next closed backend-private
lowering of the existing L2 intent and recording lease. Its decisions are:

| Semantic | Null | Vulkan | Metal | WebGPU |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Immutable intent and declarations | Validator | Preparation unavailable | Preparation unavailable | Validated issuing-device intent |
| Private processor intermediates | No GPU payload | Diagnostic failure | Diagnostic failure | Prepared canonical kernel/group with four owned buffers |
| Protected packet production | No GPU execution | Diagnostic failure | Diagnostic failure | Closed dispatch then exact 20/16-byte copies in one encoder; private execution evidence required |
| Public no-feature indexed draw | Diagnostic failure | Existing enabled-feature path only | Diagnostic failure | Diagnostic failure until producer/consumer correspondence and both fault reports pass |

WebGPU preparation consumes the exact device-issued graphics-only consumer
authorization and canonical producer package retained by the immutable intent.
It allocates metadata, a distinct four-byte fault latch, 20-byte scratch
arguments and a 16-byte scratch mapping outside recording. SnapshotMetadata/1
is built by the same typed builder as public snapshots, using the consumer
generation and actual source extent. All six producer bindings are initialized
and validated against the created device limits; parameters come from the
sealed packet. Ordinary host, writable-descriptor and transfer access to owned
buffers is sealed after preparation. Count and resident-byte reservations
conservatively include retained intent owners and reported buffer allocations,
remain charged after pool destruction, and reject rather than grow or wait.

The closed recording method must validate complete producer and consumer
declarations before mutation, obtain its encoder's opaque identity and retain
the move-only L2 lease in bounded completion storage. Only this method may
dispatch the fixed canonical kernel and copy its exact scratch outputs into
the retained packet in the same encoder. Private producer fault reporting is
mandatory before submission. Successful production/readback does not authorize
an indirect draw, authorize arbitrary protected writes, or prove consumer
execution. All four public producer execution versions stay zero until the
public consumer and command contracts have their own evidence. Existing fault
readback allocation remains a separately tracked hot-path limitation.

`tests/contract/rhi_fallback_contract_tests.cpp` is the fast contract gate.

`BindlessIndirectProcessorCommands/1` extends the same L2 intent with a
device-issued opaque processor and explicitly bounded preparation pool on the
BindlessCross extension. Recording is a primary-encoder extension command;
drawing is a fixed-record-zero, single-indexed-packet operation on the graphics
pass extension. Neither adds native handles or dedicated GpuDevice methods.
The consumer must use the exact retained authorization, snapshot, complete
plan, generation and packet mapping. Backend command retention must prove the
processor was recorded in that same encoder. Public work cannot finish/submit
without its consumer draw and both producer/consumer fault readbacks. Generation
validation merges both reports: a suppressed invalid producer is Faulted even
when its zero-instance consumer has a Clean latch. Recording identities come
from the encoder, never from application flags. Application-created processor
subclasses are not execution payloads.
The recording encoder must own the logical source and all packet resources in
its issuing RHI resource registry. Sharing a native device between distinct RHI
devices does not grant cross-device processor recording.

| Public processor semantic | Null | Vulkan | Metal | WebGPU |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Pool/payload | Deterministic execution-unavailable; L2 intent validator remains | Execution-unavailable | Execution-unavailable | Emulated closed canonical kernel; requires separately validated producer version 1 and native device limits |
| Record/draw | No GPU execution | Existing enabled-first-instance path is independent | Execution-unavailable | Same-encoder closed dispatch/copies, exact graphics consumer and protected zero-first-instance draw |
| Completion/validation | NotExecuted/Unavailable | Existing native fence reports | Execution-unavailable | Bounded actual queue retention and combined producer/consumer results |

Ordinary indirect buffers retain their original first-instance capability gate;
the packet command grants no arbitrary indirect-write or multi-draw permission.
WebGPU public version 1 is now backed by positive/negative pixel, physical
argument, mapping and fault readbacks with the created device feature enabled
and disabled. Its public `BindlessIndirectConsumer` fixture has capacity two,
one indexed record, a graphics-only consumer and exact retained authorization.
Valid logical first instance one produces physical first instance zero and a
green pixel; an invalid range produces zero instances, zero mapping, the blue
clear pixel and an aggregate Faulted report. This is scoped backend evidence,
not a WOCC workload or unrestricted indirect permission. Null/Vulkan/Metal
retain producer version zero. Fault-readback preparation and submit callbacks
still allocate; stable-frame allocation freedom and P6 remain unproved.
`src/rhi/backends/null/null_hidden_frame_smoke.cpp` remains the hidden-window /
headless smoke path for the shared L2 command flow.

## Private canonical cold preparation (l3c9, version1)

This explicit Prototype opt-in supplies storage and correspondence only. It
uses the original common packet and pool, with an unissued consumer plan;
ordinary consumer/producer-access/record lease admission deterministically
rejects PrototypeStorageOnly. Source-private parameters cannot replace the
packet's actual parameter resource. The cold six-role root uses actual native
metadata96, fault latch4, immutable source, independent scratch20/16 and packet
parameters16; readback4 remains owned alongside the latch. The real fault-domain
generation labels cold metadata only and grants no consumer authorization.
The latch awaits lease-bound clear; no initialized latch/fault result is claimed.

| New private cold semantic | Null | Vulkan | Metal | WebGPU |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Packet storage-only issuance | Logical validator only | DiagnosticFailure | Explicit Prototype; original52-byte native packet/issuer/seals/owners | New cold path DiagnosticFailure; existing public path preserved |
| Six-role metadata/fault/scratch | Shared value/builder/lease validator only | DiagnosticFailure | Explicit Prototype; queried ABI/resources, owned140-byte bounded payload | New cold path DiagnosticFailure; existing public metadata reuses shared actual-resource source |
| Consumer/producer/copy execution | Not issued | DiagnosticFailure | DiagnosticFailure | New cold path DiagnosticFailure; existing scoped public path independent |

RHI-CANONICAL-COLD-BINDINGS-PREPARATION-ONLY is the stable execution diagnostic;
RHI-CANONICAL-COLD-BINDINGS-PAYLOAD-MISSING closes Vulkan/WebGPU private cold
preparation. CPU quota, incremental byte and complete owner ceilings are
explicit; retained packet/source/pipeline allocations stay charged under their
original budgets. Native revalidation checks issuer registry, exact original
objects/control blocks, tracked storage, reported allocations, states/seals and
all six scalar ABI slots. Metadata counts come from actual resource extents and
approved strides through the shared RHI builder. No native content readback,
allocation, encoder, IO or dispatch occurs during validation. Pool destruction
preserves issuer epoch and retained budget leases. Changed native ownership,
registry, state, budgets or generation refuse deterministically; retry requires
valid roots/budgets and release of failed cold allocations. No promotion or
privileged write capability is provided.

### Retained ownership versus live native facts — implementation contract

Ownership inspection validates the exact original root, graph, registered source
lifetime, independent CPU domains, source budgets and retained owner bounds. It
may observe Prepared, owned Pending finals or Committed finals; that read-only
result grants no execution authority. Cold inspection additionally requires
Prepared, an empty journal and no encoder/finished/submitted command. The cold
seal, reservation and admission retain this restriction. Private native live
validation must use the ownership path and exact captured root/seal/reservation/
closure/command/device/queue/recording identity, require owned Pending finals,
and check encoder/finished/submitted payload consistency. Caller-supplied phase
enums or replacement ordinary completion reservations cannot stand in for facts.
Null remains ValidatorOnly and other backend execution gates remain closed until
actual issuer/callback evidence is validated. This split adds no native event API.

## Original stream compilation and selected projection traversal (2026-10-04)

`OriginalCommandStreamCompilation/1` compiles the complete supported immutable
`OriginalCommandInputs` profile into pointer-free ordered operations. Each operation
binds its original command, sparse descriptor, reference span, and family-specific
expansion ordinal. Scalar/dependency operations have no expansion; copy elements,
buffer uploads, texture upload/readback mips and attachment references use separate
ordinal spaces. Explicit descriptors and existing `TransferCopyProjection` and
upload/readback layout contracts determine counts. No extent, pitch, staging content,
native resource generation or completion identity is guessed.

Independent opted-in limits cover commands, descriptors, references, copy elements,
buffer uploads, upload/readback mips, render passes, attachment references, buffer
upload **declared** bytes, texture upload CPU pixel bytes, readback **declared** bytes,
CPU closure owners and metadata. Repeated commands charge repeated work/declared
bytes even when the same original snapshot is retained once. Source and Graph quota
aliases are rejected; materialized program metadata uses an explicitly bounded
Recording quota within the original Source's authored Recording ceiling. CPU owners
are the original Inputs closure plus the program, not native registration owners.

Existing input/order/provenance validation runs first. `PreflightReplay` validates
all operations/counts before cold allocation or the first CPU projection visitor.
The immutable program retains the exact original Inputs; it does not recreate the
root, state graph or journal. `OriginalCommandAccessValidation`, the original state
plan, native closure, issuer, scope/queue/loss bindings and independent Source,
Recording/Submission/Callback/Result/staging budgets remain separate prerequisites.
Zero disables an absent family. A budget failure publishes no partial program and
invokes no projection visitor. Visitor failure stops subsequent consumption and
invalidates the whole cold projection; partially built native work is never submitted.

`OriginalStreamSelectedProjectionTraversal/1` permits the existing private selected
CopyPass lowering to consume this allocation-free operation traversal and exact
sparse descriptor indices. Source-authored command/descriptor/reference limits,
selected admission's render-pass limit and independently authored native copy limit
form its traversal budget; upload/readback categories remain disabled. Every native
copy expansion must agree with the operation's first ordinal/count. This traversal
owns no additional retained program or callback and cannot widen the exact selected
language or either proof issuer. It creates no GPU work.

Four complete-program executor outcomes are explicit: Null is ValidatorOnly;
Vulkan, Metal and WebGPU report stable DiagnosticFailure (`*-EXECUTOR-MISSING`).
The existing private WebGPU CopyPass executor remains separately versioned and
selected. CPU compilation does not promote capability, NativeSync or generic/bindless
execution. Missing native initialized buffer staging, packed texture staging,
readback mapping/publication, draw/dispatch/query bindings and complete ordered
native executors remain unfinished.

Readiness: **Contract Complete** and **Prototype Complete** apply only to CPU stream

it does not establish complete-program **Backend Validated**, **Platform Validated**
or **Production Ready**. Selected queue/output/error/loss evidence is separately

## OrderedOriginalCopyAttachment/1 and explicit cold/lowering /2

Cold admission /2 and copy/attachment lowering /2 select this profile with explicit
Prototype opt-in. /1 preserves its exact ten-command CopyPass vocabulary and rejects
/2-only budget fields. /2 permits only original transfer-pass boundaries, four copy
families, whole texture transitions, empty attachment-pass boundaries and Finish.
Existing original input/order/access/state validators remain mandatory. Unsupported
upload/readback/draw/dispatch/query/debug/alias/buffer-barrier families fail before
GPU work; no empty native payload stands in for them.

Original commands and render passes are derived from the complete immutable stream,
with independent authored command, expanded copy, pass and attachment-reference
bounds. Exactly one real encoder and command buffer serve the complete stream.
Recording scopes3, submission scopes3, queue completion1 and runtime loss1 consume
original capacity1/8/8; original device loss1 stays separately owned and charged.
Pass descriptors/colors/depth fields are backend-private, bounded and cold-allocated
under the exact root's Recording quota. Native per-pass descriptors must retain
stable pointers after their bounded storage is finalized. No new root/journal or
callback owner is introduced. Actual foreign native wrapper byte usage remains a
Prototype limitation; bounded creation counts do not claim quota-metered JS bytes.

Every native command consumes its original ordered operation. Copy native expansion
must match the original ordinal/count. Each BeginRenderPass selects its own immutable
original attachment descriptor; EndRenderPass closes that exact native encoder.
Finish occurs only after the validated complete sequence. Cold allocations finish
before recording; no per-draw pipeline/shader creation, file/network IO or blocking
GPU wait is added. Empty transfer boundaries are L2 grouping on the same native
encoder, not fake completion. Empty attachment passes perform real load/store/clear.

Whole texture transitions validate exact complete CopySrc/CopyDst/ColorAttachment/
DepthAttachment scopes (or explicit Undefined discard) and matching aspect/range.
WebGPU's normative automatic ordered GPU usage provides Equivalent mapping on these
states; the canonical original journal owns pending/final logical states. This
mapping is not a CPU synchronization primitive and cannot promote NativeSync.
Unsupported ranges/scopes/formats/native bindings reject deterministically.

OrderedOriginalUpload/1 defines the next explicit canonical native profile. It
preserves the original complete positional stream (ordered copy/empty attachment
vocabulary plus BufferUpload, TextureUpload and exact transfer BufferBarrier).
Upload staging, native copy-command work, texture-upload command counts and
texture-destination write texel work have independent authored limits. Destination
write work is summed per upload; it is not physical/resident allocation or unique
target retention. Native source/target allocation ownership must be separately
bounded by the same-root native closure and initialized-source join.

until the full native payload is implemented and tested. Existing /1 and /2 native
profiles do not inherit uploads. No default opt-in, second journal or third issuer.

Implementation must bind each upload ordinal to the exact original registered
initialized buffer lease/payload/allocation and exclusive target expectation.
Recording scopes validate every actual target copy use before queue admission;
an unvalidated creation handle cannot authorize submit. Whole-texture content

submission scopes, invocation and queue completion publish Completed. Pre-submit

uncertainty/cancel/loss invalidates and quarantines. Native output evidence must
cover all destination bytes/mips and genuine lifecycle paths under authored
budgets, with hot allocation0, fast contract and Null/browser smoke. Target output
completion and full b4 remain open until those proofs exist.

Source maxNativePayloadObjects/maxNativeStagingBytes remain execution admission

No native creation/write/encoder/queue/callback occurs in join publication.
Prepared validation closes on pending/stale/root mismatch, changed payload/layout,
foreign registration/device, forged proof owner and quota-domain replacement. This
prerequisite cannot enable existing CopyPass/1 or OrderedCopyAttachment/1 uploads.

The backend-private native C adapter must consume actual wgpuBufferMapAsync,
wgpuBufferReadMappedRange and wgpuBufferUnmap algorithms, requiring emdawn and
GPUBuffer.mapState agreement at each access boundary. Map ranges use spec-defined
four-byte size alignment, offset0 and explicit native MapRead usage. All adapter
objects/userdata live in cold Callback-owned original slots; no hot core-owned
allocation or new RecordedCommand proof issuer is allowed. Native scope listeners
are the existing cold NativeCompletionReceipt/1 payloads. Caller/enum/boolean
cannot synthesize delivery. C-map callback reports only native map status.

Guarded native invocation must catch JS throws across a trivial C thunk. Unknown
map/read/unmap invocation fails with stable diagnostics, preserves the exact cold
slot/root/Graph, and never claims callback/lifetime completion. C callbacks may
arrive synchronously when wgpuBufferUnmap aborts a pending emdawn future; caller
must retain the original state through the entire call. Only actual callback plus
bridge/native unmap agreement, or a proven native loss teardown with all future
userdata drained, permits release. Listener suppression alone is insufficient.
Do not manually alter emdawn state/future tables. External emdawn event allocation
is opaque and hot in this library version: selected use remains explicit

map retirement is separate from independent Result publication. Facts describe
original /5 expected maps, actually invoked C maps, genuine map callbacks,

drain and genuine queue/loss evidence. These are validation inputs only; no public
facts/enum/bool may create native authority or retire the original graph.

Each issued C map must deliver its actual callback and prove bridge/native unmap
agreement before its raw userdata or original graph can retire. Every issued map
owns exactly three original scope receipt slots. Suppressed scope listeners may

listeners is not supported by the C adapter. Invocation uncertainty, cancel,
native map/scope errors and physical loss invalidate-and-retain until original
callbacks, every issued C map and safe unmap have drained. Queue suppression is
not GPU completion: MaySubmit requires genuine queue receipt or actual loss even
when all userdata is suppressed. Inconsistent/overflow facts fail closed.

execution remain deterministic DiagnosticFailure until their private native
runtime consumes this contract. Existing /4 success retirement must remain
unchanged; /5 must keep the existing State/Graph/loss/seal across mapping and use
